\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{(S)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{d=1}^{\lceil D/2\rceil}\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{(Sl)}[C_{ll}^{(Sl)}Q_{l}^{(Sl)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{(Sl)}]^{d-1}+\boldsymbol{E},

(C.13)

\displaystyle Q_{j}^{(S)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{d=1}^{\lceil D/2\rceil}Q_{j}^{(Sl)}[(B_{jl}^{(Sl)})^{2}Q_{l}^{(Sl)}Q_{j}^{(Sl)}]^{d-1}+e,

(C.14)

for remainder terms
\boldsymbol{E}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
and
e\in\mathbb{C}
satisfying,
by the bounds of Lemma
14
,

\|\boldsymbol{E}\|=O_{\prec}^{\,\mathcal{E}_{L}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(|C_{ll}^{(Sl)}|^{D/2}}}\right)=O_{\prec}^{\,\mathcal{E}_{L}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((N^{-1}\Psi)^{D}}}\right),\quad|e|=O_{\prec}^{\,\mathcal{E}_{L}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(|(B_{jl}^{(Sl)})^{2}|^{D/2}}}\right)=O_{\prec}^{\,\mathcal{E}_{L}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((N^{-1}\Psi)^{D}}}\right).

In particular,
(
C.14
) shows (
C.11
). Applying (
C.13
)
to the definitions of
Y_{i}^{(S)}[d]
and
Z_{ijk}^{(S)}[d]
, we get

\displaystyle Y_{i}^{(S)}[d]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{g}_{i}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma})\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{\lceil D/2\rceil}\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{(Sl)}[C_{ll}^{(Sl)}Q_{l}^{(Sl)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{(Sl)}]^{d^{\prime}-1}+\boldsymbol{E}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{\lceil D/2\rceil}\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{(Sl)}[C_{ll}^{(Sl)}Q_{l}^{(Sl)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{(Sl)}]^{d^{\prime}-1}+\boldsymbol{E}}}\right]}}\right)^{d},

\displaystyle Z_{ijk}^{(S)}[d]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{g}_{i}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma})\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{g}_{j}\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{\lceil D/2\rceil}\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{(Sl)}[C_{ll}^{(Sl)}Q_{l}^{(Sl)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{(Sl)}]^{d^{\prime}-1}+\boldsymbol{E}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sum_{d^{\prime}=1}^{\lceil D/2\rceil}\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{(Sl)}[C_{ll}^{(Sl)}Q_{l}^{(Sl)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{(Sl)}]^{d^{\prime}-1}+\boldsymbol{E}}}\right]}}\right)^{d}.

For any matrix
\boldsymbol{B}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
independent of
\boldsymbol{g}_{i}
, observe that

\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{g}_{i}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma})\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{B}=O_{\prec}^{\,\mathcal{E}(S)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi_{N}\|\boldsymbol{B}\|}}\right)

and
\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{g}_{i}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma})\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{g}_{j}\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{B}=O_{\prec}^{\,\mathcal{E}(S)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Psi_{N}\Phi_{N}\|\boldsymbol{B}\|}}\right)

by the same arguments as those bounding
Y_{i}^{(S)}[d]
and
Z_{ijk}^{(S)}[d]

in the proof of Lemma
14
. Then,
expanding the above and absorbing all terms containing
\boldsymbol{E}

and all terms with combined power of
C_{ll}^{(Sl)}
larger than
D/2

into
O_{\prec}^{\,\mathcal{E}(S)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(N^{-D}\Psi_{N}^{D}\Phi_{N}}}\right)
remainders, we obtain for some coefficients

\alpha(d,d^{\prime},D)\in\mathbb{R}
that

\displaystyle Y_{i}^{(S)}[d]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{g}_{i}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma})\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{A}\sum_{d^{\prime}=0}^{\lceil D/2\rceil}\alpha(d,d^{\prime},D)[C_{ll}^{(Sl)}Q_{l}^{(Sl)}]^{d^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{(Sl)}[\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{(Sl)}]^{d+d^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+O_{\prec}^{\,\mathcal{E}(S)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(N^{-D}\Psi_{N}^{D}\Phi_{N}}}\right),

\displaystyle Z_{ijk}^{(S)}[d]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{g}_{i}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma})\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{g}_{j}\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{A}\sum_{d^{\prime}=0}^{\lceil D/2\rceil}\alpha(d,d^{\prime},D)[C_{ll}^{(Sl)}Q_{l}^{(Sl)}]^{d^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{(Sl)}[\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{\Omega}^{(Sl)}]^{d+d^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+O_{\prec}^{\,\mathcal{E}(S)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(N^{-D}\Psi_{N}^{D}\Phi_{N}}}\right).

Finally, applying the Sherman-Morrison formula (
C.12
)
to expand each copy of
\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}
, and re-indexing the summations by

d+d^{\prime}\mapsto d^{\prime}
, we get (
C.7
) and (
C.8
).
∎

Lemma 16
.

Fix any
L,D\geq 1
. Uniformly over
S\subset[N]
with
|S|\leq L
and over

i\in S
, the following holds: Denote
\bar{S}=S\setminus\{i\}
. Then there exists
a collection of monomials
\mathcal{M}_{i,S}
such that

Y_{i}^{(i)}[0]
can be approximated as

\displaystyle Y_{i}^{(i)}[0]

\displaystyle=\sum_{q\in\mathcal{M}_{i,S}}q\bigg(\{Y_{i}^{(S)}[d]\}_{d\leq\lfloor D/2\rfloor},\{Z_{ijk}^{(S)}[d]\}_{j,k\in\bar{S},d\leq\lfloor D/2\rfloor},\{B_{jk}^{(S)}\}_{j\neq k\in\bar{S}},

\displaystyle\hskip 144.54pt\{C_{jk}^{(S)}\}_{j,k\in\bar{S}},\{Q_{j}^{(S)}\}_{j\in\bar{S}}\bigg)+O_{\prec}^{\,\mathcal{E}_{L}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(N^{-D}\Psi_{N}^{D}\Phi_{N}}}\right).

(C.15)

Each monomial
q\in\mathcal{M}_{i,S}
is a product of a real-valued scalar coefficient
and one or more factors of the form

Y_{i}^{(S)}[d]
,
Z_{ijk}^{(S)}[d]
,
B_{jk}^{(S)}
with
j\neq k
,

C_{jk}^{(S)}
,
Q_{j}^{(S)}
for
j,k\in\bar{S}
and
d\leq\lfloor D/2\rfloor
.
We have
q=O_{\prec}^{\,\mathcal{E}_{L}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi_{N}}}\right)
uniformly over
q\in\mathcal{M}_{i,S}
,
and the number of monomials
|\mathcal{M}_{i,S}|
is most a constant depending on

L,D
. Furthermore:

(a)

There is exactly one factor of the form
Y_{i}^{(S)}[d]
or

Z_{ijk}^{(S)}[d]
appearing in
q
.

(b)

The number of factors
Z_{ijk}^{(S)}[d]
,

B_{jk}^{(S)}
, and
C_{jk}^{(S)}
appearing in
q
is no less than the number
of distinct indices of
\bar{S}
(not including
i
) that appear as lower
indices across all factors of
q
.

Proof.

We arbitrarily order the indices of
\bar{S}=S\setminus\{i\}
as

l_{1},l_{2},\ldots,l_{|S|-1}
. Beginning with the monomial
Y_{i}^{(i)}[0]
,
iteratively for
j=1,2,\ldots,|S|-1
, we
replace all factors with superscript
(il_{1}\ldots l_{j-1})

by a sum of terms with superscript
(il_{1}\ldots l_{j})
,
using the recursions (
C.7
)–(
C.11
). It is then
direct to check that
this gives a representation of the form (
C.15
), where:

•

Each application of (
C.7
)–(
C.8
) replaces a
factor
Y_{i}^{(\ldots)}[d]
or
Z_{ijk}^{(\ldots)}[d]
by terms
having exactly one such factor. Thus, each monomial
q\in\mathcal{M}_{i,S}

has exactly one factor
Y_{i}^{(S)}[d]
or
Z_{ijk}^{(S)}[d]
.

•

The number of total applications of
(
C.7
)–(
C.11
) is bounded by a constant depending on

L,D
, so
|\mathcal{M}_{i,S}|
and the scalar coefficient of each
q\in\mathcal{M}_{i,S}

are both bounded by constants depending on
L,D
. Then, by the bounds of
(
C.5
), each
q\in\mathcal{M}_{i,S}
satisfies

q=O_{\prec}^{\,\mathcal{E}_{L}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi_{N}}}\right)
, and the remainder in (
C.15
) is at most

O_{\prec}^{\,\mathcal{E}_{L}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(N^{-D}\Psi_{N}^{D}\Phi_{N}}}\right)
. If
q
has the term
Y_{i}^{(S)}[d]
or

Z_{i}^{(S)}[d]
, then it also has combined power of

\{C_{jk}^{(S)}\}_{j,k\in\bar{S}}
equal to
d
, and hence may be
absorbed into the remainder of (
C.15
) if
d>D/2
.

•

Each term on the right side of (
C.7
)–(
C.11
)
that contains the new lower index
l
has at least one more factor of the
form
Z_{ijk}^{(\ldots)}[d]
,
B_{jk}^{(\ldots)}
, or
C_{jk}^{(\ldots)}
than the left side. Thus, each monomial
q\in\mathcal{M}_{i,S}
is such
that the number of distinct lower indices of
\bar{S}
across all of its factors
is no greater than the number of its factors of the form

Z_{ijk}^{(\ldots)}[d]
,
B_{jk}^{(\ldots)}
, or
C_{jk}^{(\ldots)}
.

Combining these observations yields the lemma.
∎

Proof of Lemma
13
.

For each
\varepsilon,D>0
, let us fix an even integer
L=L(\varepsilon,D)>D/\varepsilon
.
The assumption of this lemma guarantees

\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{E}(S)^{c}\}\prec 0
uniformly over
S\subset[N]
with
|S|\leq L
. Since the number of such subsets is at most
N^{L}
, we may take a union
bound (c.f. Proposition
7
(a))
to obtain
\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{E}_{L}^{c}\}\prec 0
for the intersection event

\mathcal{E}_{L}
of (
C.4
). Noting that

(1-\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}_{i}})[\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{\Omega}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}]=Y_{i}^{(i)}[0]
,
to prove the lemma, it suffices to show for any
\varepsilon,D>0
and
all sufficiently large
N
that

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i}^{(i)}[0]}}\right)\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{E}_{L}\}>\max\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\Psi_{N}}{N},\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}}}\right)\Phi_{N}\cdot N^{\varepsilon}}}\right]<N^{-D}.

(C.16)

In anticipation of applying Markov’s inequality, we analyze

\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i}^{(i)}[0]}}\right)^{L}\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{E}_{L}\}}}\right]=\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{L}=1}^{N}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\prod_{l=1}^{L}Y_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}[0]\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{E}_{L}\}}}\right]}_{:=\mathbb{E}[m(i_{1},\ldots,i_{L})]}.

(C.17)

Fix any index tuple
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{L})
.
Letting
S=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{L}\}
be the set of distinct indices in this tuple,
we apply Lemma
16
to each term

Y_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}[0]
, with this set
S
and with
D=L
. This gives

m(i_{1},\ldots,i_{L})=\sum_{q^{(1)}\in\mathcal{M}(i_{1},S)}\ldots\sum_{q^{(l)}\in\mathcal{M}(i_{l},S)}\prod_{l=1}^{L}q^{(l)}\cdot\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{E}_{L}\}+O_{\prec}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((N^{-1}\Psi_{N})^{L}\Phi_{N}^{L}}}\right),

(C.18)

where each
\mathcal{M}(i_{l},S)
is the collection of monomials arising in the
approximation of
Y_{i_{l}}^{(i_{l})}[0]
,
and we have applied
q^{(l)}=O_{\prec}^{\,\mathcal{E}_{L}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi_{N}}}\right)
to bound the remainder.
Observe that by (
C.6
) and Holder’s inequality, we have

\mathbb{E}[|m(i_{1},\ldots,i_{L})|^{2}]\leq N^{C}
and

\mathbb{E}[|\prod_{l=1}^{L}q^{(l)}\cdot\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{E}_{L}\}|^{2}]\leq N^{C}

for all
q^{(1)},\ldots,q^{(L)}
and a constant
C>0
.
By this and the given condition
\Psi_{N},\Phi_{N}\geq N^{-\upsilon}
,
we may take expectations in (
C.18
) using
Proposition
7
(d) to get

\mathbb{E}[m(i_{1},\ldots,i_{L})]=\sum_{q^{(1)}\in\mathcal{M}(i_{1},S)}\ldots\sum_{q^{(l)}\in\mathcal{M}(i_{l},S)}\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\prod_{l=1}^{L}q^{(l)}\cdot\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{E}_{L}\}}}\right]+O_{\prec}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((N^{-1}\Psi_{N})^{L}\Phi_{N}^{L}}}\right).

(C.19)

Now to bound
\mathbb{E}[\prod_{l=1}^{L}q^{(l)}\cdot\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{E}_{L}\}]
,
we consider separately two cases, focusing on those indices
i_{l}
which
appear exactly once in
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{L})
.
In the first case, suppose there is some such index
i_{l}
that does not appear
as a lower index of
q^{(l^{\prime})}
for any
l^{\prime}\neq l
. Fixing this set

S=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{L}\}
and index
i_{l}\in S
, let us introduce

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\bigg\{\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}\text{ exists, }\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}\leq\Phi_{N},\;\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}\|_{F}\leq\Psi_{N},\;\|(z^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}+\tilde{m}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\|\leq C_{0},

\displaystyle\hskip 144.54pt\text{ and }|1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{g}_{j}|\geq c_{0}\text{ for all }j\in S\setminus\{i_{l}\}\bigg\}.

Comparing
with the definition of
\mathcal{E}(S)
from (
C.2
), observe that
only the last condition defining
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
is different (where we do not require
the bound for
j=i_{l}
), so that this event
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
is independent of
\boldsymbol{g}_{i_{l}}
.
Then
\mathcal{E}_{L}\subseteq\mathcal{E}(S)\subseteq\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
, and

\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\prod_{l=1}^{L}q^{(l)}\cdot\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{E}_{L}\}}}\right]=\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\prod_{l=1}^{L}q^{(l)}\cdot\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\}}}\right]-\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\prod_{l=1}^{L}q^{(l)}\cdot\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{E}_{L}^{c}\}}}\right].

(C.20)

For the first term of (
C.20
), observe that
both
\{q^{(l^{\prime})}:l^{\prime}\neq l\}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
are independent of

\boldsymbol{g}_{i_{l}}
, and only the one
factor
Y_{i_{l}}^{(S)}[d]
or
Z_{i_{l}jk}^{(S)}[d]
in
q^{(l)}
depends on

\boldsymbol{g}_{i_{l}}
. Then, noting that
\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}_{i}}[Y_{i}^{(S)}[d]]=0
and

\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{g}_{i}}[Z_{ijk}^{(S)}[d]]=0
, the first term of (
C.20
)
is 0. For the second term of (
C.20
),
observe that all statements of (
C.6
) continue to hold with

\mathcal{E}(S)
replaced by
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
, except for the bound on
Q_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
.
But
Q_{i_{l}}^{(S)}
appears neither in

\{q^{(l^{\prime})}:l^{\prime}\neq l\}
nor in
q^{(l)}
, so we may apply Holder’s inequality
to get
\mathbb{E}[|\prod_{l=1}^{L}q^{(l)}|^{2}\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\}]\leq N^{C}
for a constant

C>0
. Then, applying Cauchy-Schwarz and
\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{E}_{L}^{c}\}\prec 0
, the second
term of (
C.20
) is bounded by
N^{-D^{\prime}}
for any fixed constant

D^{\prime}>0
and all large
N
. Thus,

\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\prod_{l=1}^{L}q^{(l)}\cdot\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{E}_{L}\}}}\right]\leq N^{-D^{\prime}}.

(C.21)

In the second case,
every index
i_{l}
that appears exactly once in
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{L})
appears
as a lower index of
q^{(l^{\prime})}
for some
l^{\prime}\neq l
. Call the number
of such indices
K
. Then condition (b) of Lemma
16
implies
that the total number of factors of the forms

Z_{ijk}^{(S)}[d]
,
B_{jk}^{(S)}
for
j\neq k
, and
C_{jk}^{(S)}

across all monomials
q^{(1)},\ldots,q^{(L)}
is at least
K
. Then,
by the bounds of Lemma
14
and
Proposition
7
(d), we have

\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\prod_{l=1}^{L}q^{(l)}\cdot\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{E}_{L}\}}}\right]\prec(N^{-1}\Psi_{N})^{K}\Phi_{N}^{L}.

(C.22)

Under the given condition
\Phi_{N},\Psi_{N}\geq N^{-\upsilon}
, we have

N^{-D^{\prime}}\leq(N^{-1}\Psi_{N})^{K}\Phi_{N}^{L}
for large enough
D^{\prime}
. Then,
combining the two cases (
C.21
) and (
C.22
) and applying
this back to (
C.19
), we get

\mathbb{E}[m(i_{1},\ldots,i_{L})]\prec(N^{-1}\Psi_{N})^{K}\Phi_{N}^{L}

(C.23)

where
K
is the number of indices in
S=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{L}\}
that appear exactly
once in
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{L})
.
Let
J
be the number of distinct indices in
S=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{L}\}
that appear
at least twice in
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{L})
. Then
2J+K\leq L
, and the number of
index tuples
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{L})\in[N]^{L}
with these values of
(J,K)
is at
most
CN^{J+K}
, for a constant
C=C(J,K)>0
. Then,
applying (
C.23
) back to (
C.17
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i}^{(i)}[0]}}\right)^{L}\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{E}_{L}\}}}\right]

\displaystyle\prec\max_{J,K\geq 0:\,2J+K\leq L}N^{J+K}\cdot(N^{-1}\Psi_{N})^{K}\Phi_{N}^{L}

\displaystyle=\max_{J,K\geq 0:\,2J+K\leq L}(\sqrt{N})^{2J}\Psi_{N}^{K}\Phi_{N}^{L}\leq\max(\Psi_{N},\sqrt{N})^{L}\Phi_{N}^{L}.

Finally, by Markov’s inequality, the probability in (
C.16
) is at most

\displaystyle\max(\Psi_{N},\sqrt{N})^{-L}\Phi_{N}^{-L}N^{-\varepsilon L}\cdot\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}Y_{i}^{(i)}[0]}}\right)^{L}\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{E}_{L}\}}}\right]\prec N^{-\varepsilon L},

and (
C.16
) follows as desired under our initial
choice
L=L(\varepsilon,D)>D/\varepsilon
.
∎

C.2
No eigenvalues outside the support

We now prove Theorem
9
.
Let
m_{N}(z),\tilde{m}_{N}(z)
be the Stieltjes
transform of the
N
-dependent deterministic measures
\mu_{N},\tilde{\mu}_{N}
.
For each
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
,
\tilde{m}_{N}(z)
is the unique root in
\mathbb{C}^{+}
to the equation

\displaystyle z

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\tilde{m}_{N}(z)}+\gamma_{N}\int\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda\tilde{m}_{N}(z)}d\nu_{N}(\lambda),

(C.24)

and
m_{N}(z),\tilde{m}_{N}(z)
are related by

\tilde{m}_{N}(z)=\gamma_{N}m_{N}(z)+(1-\gamma_{N})({-}1/z)
.
Define the discrete set

\mathcal{T}_{N}=\{0\}\cup\{-1/\lambda:\lambda\in\mathrm{supp}({\nu_{N}})\}.

(C.25)

On the domain
\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathcal{T}_{N}
,
we may define the formal inverse of (
C.24
),

\displaystyle z_{N}(\tilde{m})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\tilde{m}}+\gamma_{N}\int\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda\tilde{m}}d\nu_{N}(\lambda),

(C.26)

which is a finite-
N
analogue of (
A.4
).
Let
\mathcal{S}_{N}
be the deterministic support defined in (
B.2
), and let

U_{N}(\varepsilon)
be the spectral domain (
B.3
). The following basic
properties of
\mathcal{S}_{N}
and
\tilde{m}_{N}(z)
are known.

Proposition 17
.

Suppose Assumption
5
(a) holds, and fix any
\varepsilon>0
. Then there
exist constants
C_{0},c_{0}>0
, depending only on
\varepsilon
and the constants
C,c
of
Assumption
5
(a), such that for all
x\in\mathcal{S}_{N}
we have
|x|\leq C
,
and for all
z=x+i\eta\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)
we have

c<|\tilde{m}_{N}(z)|<C,\qquad c\eta\leq|\operatorname{Im}\tilde{m}_{N}(z)|\leq C\eta,\qquad\min_{\lambda\in\mathrm{supp}({\nu_{N}})}|1+\lambda\,\tilde{m}_{N}(z)|\geq c

Proof.

See
[
FJ22
, Propositions A.3, B.1, B.2]
.
∎

Let
m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}(z)=N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}(\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{K}-z\boldsymbol{I})^{-1}
be the Stieltjes
transform of the empirical eigenvalue distribution of
\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{K}=\boldsymbol{G}\boldsymbol{G}^{\top}
.
Since
\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{K}
and
\boldsymbol{K}=\boldsymbol{G}^{\top}\boldsymbol{G}
have the same
eigenvalues up to
|N-n|
0’s, we have

m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}(z)=\gamma_{N}m_{\boldsymbol{K}}(z)+(1-\gamma_{N})(-1/z),

(C.27)

so in particular
m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}
coincides with
\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(\emptyset)}

from (
C.1
).
We begin with a preliminary estimate for the Stieltjes transform

m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}(z)
when
\operatorname{Im}z\geq N^{-1/11}
. Similar statements have been shown
in
[
Sil95
,
BS98
]
, and we provide an argument
here following ideas of
[
BS98
, Section 3]
for later reference.

Lemma 18
.

Fix any
\varepsilon>0
, and suppose Assumption
5
holds.
Then, uniformly over
z=x+i\eta\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)
with
\operatorname{Im}z\geq N^{-1/11}
,

m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}(z)-\tilde{m}_{N}(z)\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}\eta^{4}}.

Proof.

Let
\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}
and
\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}
be as defined in
(
C.1
) with
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}=z\boldsymbol{I}
.
Applying the Sherman-Morrison formula

\boldsymbol{R}=\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}-\frac{N^{-1}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}}{1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}},

(C.28)

for any matrix
\boldsymbol{B}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{B}=\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{K}-z\boldsymbol{I})\boldsymbol{R}\boldsymbol{B}

\displaystyle=-z\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{R}\boldsymbol{B}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}\boldsymbol{B}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}

\displaystyle=-z\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{R}\boldsymbol{B}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{B}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}}{1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}}.

(C.29)

Choosing
\boldsymbol{B}=\boldsymbol{I}
in (
C.29
),
applying
\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{R}=n\,m_{\boldsymbol{K}}=N\,m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}+(n-N)(-1/z)
, and rearranging,
we obtain the identity

m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}=-\frac{1}{Nz}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}}.

(C.30)

Now fix any deterministic matrix
\boldsymbol{A}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
, define

d_{i}=\frac{1}{N}\,\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{A}(\boldsymbol{I}+m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}-\frac{1}{N}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{R}\boldsymbol{A}(\boldsymbol{I}+m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Sigma},

and choose
\boldsymbol{B}=\boldsymbol{A}(\boldsymbol{I}+m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}

in (
C.29
). Then, applying also the identity
(
C.30
), we get

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{A}(\boldsymbol{I}+m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}

\displaystyle=-z\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{R}\boldsymbol{A}(\boldsymbol{I}+m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}-zm_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{R}\boldsymbol{A}(\boldsymbol{I}+m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{d_{i}}{1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}}

\displaystyle=-z\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{R}\boldsymbol{A}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{d_{i}}{1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}}.

(C.31)

We proceed to bound
d_{i}
, where (for later purposes)
we derive estimates in terms of the Frobenius norms of

\boldsymbol{R},\boldsymbol{R}\boldsymbol{A},\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)},\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{A}
rather than their operator norms.
Note that Assumption
5
(c) implies, for any matrix

\boldsymbol{B}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
independent of
\boldsymbol{g}_{i}
,

\|\boldsymbol{B}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}\|^{2}=\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\boldsymbol{B}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}\prec\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\boldsymbol{B}+\|\boldsymbol{B}^{*}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}\prec\|\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}^{2}.

(C.32)

We have also, by Assumption
5
(c) and the Sherman-Morrison
formula (
C.28
),

\displaystyle N^{-1}|\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{R}\boldsymbol{B}-\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{B}|

\displaystyle=N^{-2}|1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}|^{-1}|\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{B}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}|

\displaystyle\prec N^{-2}|1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}|^{-1}\Big(|\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{B}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}|+\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{B}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\|_{F}\Big)

\displaystyle\prec N^{-2}|1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}|^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\|_{F}.

(C.33)

Define

d_{i}=d_{i,1}+d_{i,2}+d_{i,3}+d_{i,4}
where

\displaystyle d_{i,1}

\displaystyle=N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{A}(\boldsymbol{I}+m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}-N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{A}(\boldsymbol{I}+\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i},

(C.34)

\displaystyle d_{i,2}

\displaystyle=N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{A}(\boldsymbol{I}+\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}-N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{A}(\boldsymbol{I}+\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1},

\displaystyle d_{i,3}

\displaystyle=N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{A}(\boldsymbol{I}+\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}-N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}\boldsymbol{A}(\boldsymbol{I}+\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1},

\displaystyle d_{i,4}

\displaystyle=N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}\boldsymbol{A}(\boldsymbol{I}+\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}-N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}\boldsymbol{A}(\boldsymbol{I}+m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}.

Applying the identity
\boldsymbol{A}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{B}^{-1}=\boldsymbol{A}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{B}-\boldsymbol{A})\boldsymbol{B}^{-1}
,
the definition of
\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}
in (
C.1
),
and the bounds (
C.32
) and (
C.33
) (the latter with

\boldsymbol{B}=\boldsymbol{I}
),

\displaystyle|d_{i,1}|

\displaystyle\leq N^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{A}\|\|(\boldsymbol{I}+m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\|\|(\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}-m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}})\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\|\|(\boldsymbol{I}+\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\|\|\boldsymbol{g}_{i}\|

\displaystyle\prec N^{-5/2}|1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}|^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\|_{F}^{2}\|(\boldsymbol{I}+m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\|\|(\boldsymbol{I}+\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\|.

(C.35)

Applying Assumption
5
(c),

\displaystyle|d_{i,2}|

\displaystyle\prec N^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{A}(\boldsymbol{I}+\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\|_{F}\leq N^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}\|(\boldsymbol{I}+\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\|.

(C.36)

Applying the Sherman-Morrison identity (
C.28
),

|\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{u}\boldsymbol{v}^{\top}|\leq\|\boldsymbol{u}\|\|\boldsymbol{v}\|
, and (
C.32
),

\displaystyle|d_{i,3}|

\displaystyle\leq N^{-2}|1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}|^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}\|\|\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}(\boldsymbol{I}+\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\|

\displaystyle\prec N^{-2}|1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}|^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\|_{F}\|(\boldsymbol{I}+\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\|.

(C.37)

Finally, applying
\boldsymbol{A}^{-1}-\boldsymbol{B}^{-1}=\boldsymbol{A}^{-1}(\boldsymbol{B}-\boldsymbol{A})\boldsymbol{B}^{-1}
,
(
C.33
) (with
\boldsymbol{B}=\boldsymbol{I}
), and

|\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{B}|\leq\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}\|\boldsymbol{B}\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{N}\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}\|\boldsymbol{B}\|
,

\displaystyle|d_{i,4}|

\displaystyle=N^{-1}\Big|\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}\boldsymbol{A}(\boldsymbol{I}+\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}(\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}-m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}})\boldsymbol{\Sigma}(\boldsymbol{I}+m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\Big|

\displaystyle\prec N^{-5/2}|1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}|^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{R}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}\|\boldsymbol{R}\|_{F}^{2}\|(\boldsymbol{I}+\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\|\|(\boldsymbol{I}+m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\|.

(C.38)

For the current proof, we apply (
C.31
) and the definitions
(
C.34
) with
\boldsymbol{A}=\boldsymbol{I}
.
Recalling
\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{R}=n\,m_{\boldsymbol{K}}=N\,m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}+(n-N)(-1/z)
and
rearranging (
C.31
) with
\boldsymbol{A}=\boldsymbol{I}
, we get the identity

z_{N}(m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}})-z={-}\frac{1}{m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}}\cdot\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{d_{i}}{1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}}

(C.39)

where
z_{N}(m)=-(1/m)+N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}(\boldsymbol{I}+m\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}
is the function
defined in (
C.26
). For any
z=x+i\eta
with
\eta>0
, we have

\displaystyle|z(1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i})|

\displaystyle\geq\operatorname{Im}[z(1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i})]\geq\operatorname{Im}z=\eta,

(C.40)

\displaystyle\max(\|\boldsymbol{R}\|_{F},\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\|_{F})

\displaystyle\leq N^{1/2}\max(\|\boldsymbol{R}\|,\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\|)\leq N^{1/2}\eta^{-1}.

(C.41)

Here, the second inequalities of both (
C.40
) and
(
C.41
) follow from the spectral representations of

\boldsymbol{R},\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}
, i.e. writing
(\lambda_{j},\boldsymbol{v}_{j})_{j=1}^{n}
for the
eigenvalues and unit eigenvectors of
\boldsymbol{K}^{(i)}
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Im}[z\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Im}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[z\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\lambda_{j}-z}\boldsymbol{v}_{j}\boldsymbol{v}_{j}^{\top}}}\right)\boldsymbol{g}_{i}}}\right]=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\operatorname{Im}\frac{z}{\lambda_{j}-z}\cdot(\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{v}_{j})^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\lambda_{j}\operatorname{Im}z}{|\lambda_{j}-z|^{2}}\cdot(\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{v}_{j})^{2}\geq 0,

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\|

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\lambda_{j}-z}\boldsymbol{v}_{j}\boldsymbol{v}_{j}^{\top}}}\right\|=\max_{j=1}^{n}|\lambda_{j}-z|^{-1}\leq\eta^{-1},

and similarly for
\|\boldsymbol{R}\|
. In particular, (
C.40
) and
(
C.41
) imply

(1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i})^{-1}\prec\eta^{-1},\qquad\|\boldsymbol{R}\|_{F},\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\|_{F}\prec N^{1/2}\eta^{-1}.

(C.42)

Next, observe that if
m(z)=\int\frac{1}{\lambda-z}d\mu(\lambda)
is the Stieltjes transform of any probability
measure
\mu
supported on
[-B,B]
, then for
z=x+i\eta
with
\eta>0
and

|z|\leq\varepsilon^{-1}
, we have

\operatorname{Im}m(z)=\int\frac{\eta}{|\lambda-z|^{2}}d\mu(\lambda)\geq c\eta,\quad|\operatorname{Re}m(z)|\leq\int\frac{|\lambda-x|}{|\lambda-z|^{2}}d\mu(\lambda)\leq(C/\eta)\operatorname{Im}m(z)

for some constants
C,c>0
depending on
\varepsilon,B
.
Consequently, for any
\lambda\geq 0
, either

\lambda\cdot|\operatorname{Re}m(z)|<1/2
or
\lambda\cdot\operatorname{Im}m(z)\geq 2\eta/C
,
so
|1+\lambda m(z)|\geq\max(2,2\eta/C)
. By Assumption
5
(b)
and Weyl’s inequality, we have
\mathbf{1}\{\|\boldsymbol{K}\|>B\}\prec 0
and

\mathbf{1}\{\|\boldsymbol{K}^{(i)}\|>B\}\prec 0
, and on the event where
\|\boldsymbol{K}\|,\|\boldsymbol{K}^{(i)}\|\leq B
, we have that
m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}},\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}
are Stieltjes
transforms of probability measures supported on
[-B,B]
. Thus, this implies

|m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}|^{-1}\leq|\operatorname{Im}m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}|^{-1}\prec\eta^{-1},\quad\max(\|(\boldsymbol{I}+m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\|,\|(\boldsymbol{I}+\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\|)\prec\eta^{-1}.

(C.43)

Applying these bounds (
C.42
) and
(
C.43
) to (
C.35
)–(
C.38
),
we get
d_{i}\prec N^{-1}\eta^{-6}+N^{-1/2}\eta^{-2}\leq 2N^{-1/2}\eta^{-2}
for

\eta\geq N^{-1/11}
. Then, applying these bounds
(
C.42
) and (
C.43
) also to
(
C.39
), we get

z_{N}(m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}})-z\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}\eta^{4}}.

(C.44)

The proof is completed by the following stability argument: When

\eta\geq N^{-1/11}
, we have
1/(\sqrt{N}\eta^{4})\ll\eta=\operatorname{Im}z
,
so (
C.44
) implies in particular that

\mathbf{1}\{z_{N}(m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}})\notin\mathbb{C}^{+}\}\prec 0.

(C.45)

On the event
z_{N}(m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}})\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
, recalling the implicit definition
of
\tilde{m}_{N}:\mathbb{C}^{+}\to\mathbb{C}^{+}
by (
C.24
),
the value
\tilde{m}_{N}(z_{N}(m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}))
must be the unique root

u\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
to the equation

z_{N}(m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}})=-\frac{1}{u}+\gamma_{N}\int\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda u}d\nu_{N}(\lambda),

i.e. to the equation
z_{N}(m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}})=z_{N}(u)
. This equation is
satisfied by
u=m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
, so we deduce that

\tilde{m}_{N}(z_{N}(m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}))=m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}
.
Then, applying that
z\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)
and that
\tilde{m}_{N}:\mathbb{C}^{+}\to\mathbb{C}^{+}

is
(4/\varepsilon^{2})
-Lipschitz over the domain
U_{N}(\varepsilon/2)
,
we obtain from (
C.44
) that

\mathbf{1}\{z_{N}(m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}})\in\mathbb{C}^{+}\}\Big(m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}-\tilde{m}_{N}(z)\Big)=\mathbf{1}\{z_{N}(m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}})\in\mathbb{C}^{+}\}\Big(\tilde{m}_{N}(z_{N}(m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}))-\tilde{m}_{N}(z)\Big)\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}\eta^{4}}.

Together with (
C.45
), this yields the lemma.
∎

Corollary 19
.

Fix any
\varepsilon>0
, and suppose Assumption
5
holds.
Then there is a constant
C>0
such that uniformly over
z\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)

with
\operatorname{Im}z\geq N^{-1/11}
,

\mathbf{1}\{\|\boldsymbol{R}(z)\|_{F}>C\sqrt{N}\}\prec 0.

Proof.

Since
m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}(z)=\gamma_{N}m_{\boldsymbol{K}}(z)+(1-\gamma_{N})(-1/z)
and

\tilde{m}_{N}(z)=\gamma_{N}m_{N}(z)+(1-\gamma_{N})(-1/z)
, Lemma

18
implies also

m_{\boldsymbol{K}}(z)-m_{N}(z)\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}\eta^{4}}\ll\eta.

Observe that

\operatorname{Im}m_{N}(z)=\int\eta/|\lambda-z|^{2}d\mu_{N}(\lambda)\leq\eta\varepsilon^{-2}

for
z\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)
, so

\mathbf{1}\{\operatorname{Im}m_{\boldsymbol{K}}(z)>(1+\varepsilon^{-2})\eta\}\prec 0
.
Then by the identity
\|\boldsymbol{R}(z)\|_{F}^{2}=\sum_{i}1/|z-\lambda_{i}(\boldsymbol{K})|^{2}=(n/\eta)\operatorname{Im}m_{\boldsymbol{K}}(z)
, we get

\mathbf{1}\{\|\boldsymbol{R}(z)\|_{F}>C\sqrt{N}\}\prec 0
for a constant
C=C(\varepsilon)>0
, as desired.
∎

We may now apply Corollary
19
and the fluctuation averaging
result of Lemma
13

to improve the estimate of Lemma
18
to the following
result.

Lemma 20
.

Fix any
\varepsilon>0
, and suppose Assumption
5
holds.
Then, uniformly over
z=x+i\eta\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)
with
\operatorname{Im}z\geq N^{-1/11}
,

m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}(z)-\tilde{m}_{N}(z)\prec\frac{1}{N}.

Proof.

We derive an improved estimate for (
C.39
). First,
combining Lemma
18
with the bounds for
\tilde{m}_{N}(z)

in Proposition
17
, there are constants
C_{0},c_{0}>0
for which

\mathbf{1}\{|m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}|>C_{0}\}\prec 0,\qquad\mathbf{1}\{|m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}|<c_{0}\}\prec 0,\qquad\mathbf{1}\{\|(\boldsymbol{I}+m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\|>C_{0}\}\prec 0

(C.46)

uniformly over
z\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)
with
\operatorname{Im}z\geq N^{-1/11}
. Next, applying
Assumption
5
(c), we have also uniformly over
i\in[N]
,

\displaystyle N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}

\displaystyle=N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}+O_{\prec}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(N^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\|_{F}}}\right)

\displaystyle=N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}+O_{\prec}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(N^{-2}|1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}|^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\|_{F}^{2}}}\right)+O_{\prec}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(N^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\|_{F}}}\right)

where the second line follows from (
C.33
) applied with

\boldsymbol{B}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}
. Applying
\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\|_{F}\prec N^{1/2}
by Corollary

19
and the estimate
|1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}|^{-1}\prec\eta^{-1}
from (
C.42
), this gives

1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}=1+N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}+O_{\prec}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(N^{-1/2}}}\right).

(C.47)

Then, applying this and

|1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}|^{-1}\prec\eta^{-1}

to (
C.30
),

m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}=-\frac{1}{z}\cdot\frac{1}{1+N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}}+O_{\prec}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(N^{-1/2}\eta^{-2}}}\right).

Together with the first bound of (
C.46
) and the bound
|z|\leq\varepsilon^{-1}
for
z\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)
, this implies
for a constant
c_{0}>0
that
\mathbf{1}\{|1+N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}|<c_{0}\}\prec 0
,
and thus
\mathbf{1}\{|1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}|<c_{0}\}\prec 0
.

Applying Corollary
19
and the above arguments now
for
\boldsymbol{K}^{(S)}
and
\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}
in place of
\boldsymbol{K}

and
\boldsymbol{R}
, we obtain for any fixed
L\geq 1
and
some constants
C_{0},c_{0}>0
, uniformly over
S\subset[N]
with
|S|\leq L
,
over
i\in S
, and over
z\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)
with
\operatorname{Im}z\geq N^{-1/11}
,

\begin{gathered}\mathbf{1}\{|\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}|>C_{0}\}\prec 0,\quad\mathbf{1}\{|\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}|<c_{0}\}\prec 0,\quad\mathbf{1}\{\|(\boldsymbol{I}+\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\|>C_{0}\}\prec 0,\\
\mathbf{1}\{\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}\|_{F}>C\sqrt{N}\}\prec 0,\quad\mathbf{1}\{|1+N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}|<c_{0}\}\prec 0,\\
\mathbf{1}\{|1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}|<c_{0}\}\prec 0.\end{gathered}

(C.48)

(We remark that a direct application of the above arguments for
\boldsymbol{K}^{(S)}

yields the first three estimates of (
C.48
) for the
quantity
\frac{N}{N-|S|}\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}=\frac{1}{N-|S|}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}+\frac{n}{N-|S|}(-1/z)
in place of
\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}
,
and the estimates for
\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}
then follow for slightly modified
constants
C_{0},c_{0}>0
because
|S|\leq L
.)

Finally, applying (
C.47
) and (
C.48
)
back to (
C.39
) and (
C.35
)–(
C.38
) with

\boldsymbol{A}=\boldsymbol{I}
, we get
|d_{i,1}|,|d_{i,3}|,|d_{i,4}|\prec N^{-1}
,

|d_{i,2}|\prec N^{-1/2}
, and

\displaystyle|z_{N}(m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}})-z|

\displaystyle\prec\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{d_{i,2}}{1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}}}}\right|+O_{\prec}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(N^{-1}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\cdot\frac{1}{1+N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}d_{i,2}}}\right|+O_{\prec}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(N^{-1}}}\right).

The statements of (
C.48
) verify the needed assumptions
of Lemma
13
with
\boldsymbol{A}=\boldsymbol{I}
,
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}=z\boldsymbol{I}
, and

\Phi_{N}=\Psi_{N}=C\sqrt{N}
. Then Lemma
13

gives
\sum_{i=1}^{N}d_{i,2}\prec 1
, and hence

|z_{N}(m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}})-z|\prec N^{-1}.

The proof is then completed by the same stability argument as in the conclusion
of the proof of Lemma
18
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
9
.

We apply the idea of
[
BS98
, Section 6]
. Let
z=x+i\eta
, where

\textnormal{dist}(x,\mathcal{S}_{N})\geq\varepsilon
and
\eta=N^{-1/11}
.
Taking imaginary part in the estimate
m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}(z)-\tilde{m}_{N}(z)\prec N^{-1}
of Lemma
20
and multiplying by

\eta
gives

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{\eta^{2}}{(\lambda_{j}(\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{K})-x)^{2}+\eta^{2}}-\int\frac{\eta^{2}}{(\lambda-x)^{2}+\eta^{2}}d\tilde{\mu}_{N}(\lambda)\prec\frac{\eta}{N}.

Fix any integer
P\geq 1
, and apply this instead
at the point
z=x+i\sqrt{p}\,\eta
for each
p=1,\ldots,P
. Then

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{\eta^{2}}{(\lambda_{j}(\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{K})-x)^{2}+p\eta^{2}}-\int\frac{\eta^{2}}{(\lambda-x)^{2}+p\eta^{2}}d\tilde{\mu}_{N}(\lambda)\prec\frac{\eta}{N}\text{ for all }p=1,\ldots,P.

Taking successive finite differences using

\frac{1}{r-q+1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\prod_{p=q}^{r}(\lambda-x)^{2}+p\eta^{2}}-\frac{1}{\prod_{p=q+1}^{r+1}(\lambda-x)^{2}+p\eta^{2}}}}\right)=\frac{\eta^{2}}{\prod_{p=q}^{r+1}(\lambda-x)^{2}+p\eta^{2}},

we then obtain

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{\eta^{2P}}{\prod_{p=1}^{P}[(\lambda_{j}(\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{K})-x)^{2}+p\eta^{2}]}-\int\frac{\eta^{2P}}{\prod_{p=1}^{P}[(\lambda-x)^{2}+p\eta^{2}]}d\tilde{\mu}_{N}(\lambda)\prec\frac{\eta}{N}.

(C.49)

Since
\textnormal{dist}(x,\mathcal{S}_{N})\geq\varepsilon
, the second integral term of
(
C.49
) is bounded by
C\eta^{2P}
for a
constant
C:=C(\varepsilon,P)>0
. Thus, we get

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbf{1}\{\lambda_{j}(\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{K})\in(x-\eta,x+\eta)\}\leq\frac{C}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\frac{\eta^{2P}}{\prod_{p=1}^{P}[(\lambda_{j}(\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{K})-x)^{2}+p\eta^{2}]}\prec\frac{\eta}{N}+\eta^{2P}

where the first inequality holds
for a constant
C:=C(P)>0
. Finally, recalling
\eta=N^{-1/11}
and taking
any
P\geq 6
, we get
\eta/N+\eta^{2P}\ll 1/N
, hence

\mathbf{1}\big\{\text{there exists an eigenvalue of }\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{K}\text{ in }(x-\eta,x+\eta)\big\}\prec 0.

Recalling Assumption
5
(b) and
taking a union bound over
x
belonging to a
\eta
-net of

[-B,B]\setminus(\mathcal{S}_{N}+(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon))
(with cardinality at most
CN^{1/11}
),
we obtain

\mathbf{1}\big\{\text{there exists an eigenvalue of }\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{K}\text{ in }\mathcal{S}_{N}+(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)\big\}\prec 0.

The theorem follows from the observation that
\boldsymbol{K}
has the same non-zero
eigenvalues as
\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{K}
, and all 0 eigenvalues belong by definition to

\mathcal{S}_{N}
.
∎

C.3
Deterministic equivalent for the
resolvent

In this section, we prove Theorem
10
.

Lemma 21
.

Suppose Assumption
5
holds. Let

\gamma_{N}^{(S)}=\frac{n}{N-|S|},\qquad\mu_{N}^{(S)}=\rho_{\gamma_{N}^{(S)}}^{\textnormal{MP}}\boxtimes\nu_{N},\qquad\tilde{\mu}_{N}^{(S)}=\gamma_{N}^{(S)}\mu_{N}^{(S)}+(1-\gamma_{N}^{(S)})\delta_{0}

be the analogues of
\gamma_{N},\mu_{N},\tilde{\mu}_{N}
defined with the dimension

N-|S|
in place of
N
.
Then for any fixed
\varepsilon>0
and
L\geq 1
, all large
N
, and all

S\subset[N]
with
|S|\leq L
,

\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}_{N}^{(S)}})\subseteq\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}_{N}})+(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{T}_{N}
and
z_{N}:\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathcal{T}_{N}\to\mathbb{C}
be as defined by
(
C.25
) and (
C.26
). Define similarly

z_{N}^{(S)}(\tilde{m})=-\frac{1}{\tilde{m}}+\gamma_{N}^{(S)}\int\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda\tilde{m}}d\nu_{N}(\lambda),\qquad z_{N}^{(S)}:\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathcal{T}_{N}\to\mathbb{C}.

We recall from Proposition
8
that

x\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}_{N}})
if and only if there exists

\tilde{m}\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\mathcal{T}_{N}
where
z_{N}(\tilde{m})=x
and

z_{N}^{\prime}(\tilde{m})>0
; the analogous characterization holds for

\mathbb{R}\setminus\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}_{N}^{(S)}})
and
z_{N}^{(S)}(\tilde{m})
.

Now fix any
\varepsilon,L>0
. By Proposition
17
, there is a constant

C_{0}>0
such that
\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}_{N}^{(S)}})\subseteq[-C_{0},C_{0}]
for all
|S|\leq L
and all large
N
. Consider any
x\in[-C_{0},C_{0}]\setminus(\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}_{N}})+(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon))
. Then
[x-\varepsilon/2,x+\varepsilon/2]\subset\mathbb{R}\setminus\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}_{N}})
, so
\tilde{m}_{N}
is well-defined and increasing on

[x-\varepsilon/2,x+\varepsilon/2]
.
Define
[\tilde{m}_{-},\tilde{m}_{+}]=[\tilde{m}_{N}(x-\varepsilon/2),\tilde{m}_{N}(x+\varepsilon/2)]
.
Then Proposition
8
implies that
z_{N}
is increasing on

[\tilde{m}_{-},\tilde{m}_{+}]
, and
z_{N}([\tilde{m}_{-},\tilde{m}_{+}])=[x-\varepsilon/2,x+\varepsilon/2]
.
Again by Proposition
17
, there is a constant
c>0
such that,
for any such
x\in[-C_{0},C_{0}]\setminus(\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}_{N}})+(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon))
, we
have

\min_{y\in[x-\varepsilon/2,x+\varepsilon/2]}\min_{\lambda\in\mathrm{supp}({\nu_{N}})}|1+\lambda\tilde{m}_{N}(y)|>c.

This then implies that there is a constant
C>0
for which

|z_{N}^{(S)}(\tilde{m})-z_{N}(\tilde{m})|=|\gamma_{N}^{(S)}-\gamma_{N}|\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\int\frac{\lambda}{1+\lambda\tilde{m}}d\nu_{N}(\lambda)}}\right|\leq\frac{C}{N}<\varepsilon/2

for all
\tilde{m}\in[\tilde{m}_{-},\tilde{m}_{+}]
,

|S|\leq L
, and large
N
. Then
z_{N}^{(S)}(\tilde{m}_{-})<z_{N}(\tilde{m}_{-})+\varepsilon/2=x

and
z_{N}^{(S)}(\tilde{m}_{+})>z_{N}(\tilde{m}_{+})-\varepsilon/2=x
.

[
SC95
, Theorem 4.3]
shows that if

m_{1},m_{2}\in[\tilde{m}_{-},\tilde{m}_{+}]
satisfy
{z_{N}^{(S)}}^{\prime}(m_{1})\geq 0
and

{z_{N}^{(S)}}^{\prime}(m_{2})\geq 0
, then
{z_{N}^{(S)}}^{\prime}(m)>0
strictly for all
m\in[m_{1},m_{2}]
. By this and the continuity and differentiability of

z_{N}^{(S)}
on
[\tilde{m}_{-},\tilde{m}_{+}]
, there must be a point
\tilde{m}\in(\tilde{m}_{-},\tilde{m}_{+})
where
z_{N}^{(S)}(\tilde{m})=x
and
{z_{N}^{(S)}}^{\prime}(\tilde{m})>0

strictly. Then Proposition
8
implies that

x\notin\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}_{N}^{(S)}})
. This holds for all

x\in[-C_{0},C_{0}]\setminus(\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}_{N}})+(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon))
, implying

\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}_{N}^{(S)}})\subseteq\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}_{N}})+(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)
as desired.
∎

The following now applies Lemma
21
and
Theorem
9
to extend the
estimates (
C.48
) previously obtained over

\{z\in U_{N}(\varepsilon):\operatorname{Im}z\geq N^{-1/11}\}
to all of
U_{N}(\varepsilon)
.

Lemma 22
.

Fix any
\varepsilon>0
and
L\geq 1
. Then for some constants
C_{0},c_{0}>0
, uniformly
over
z\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)
,
S\subset[N]
with
|S|\leq L
, and
i\in S
,
we have

\begin{gathered}\mathbf{1}\{|\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(z)|>C_{0}\}\prec 0,\quad\mathbf{1}\{|\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(z)|<c_{0}\}\prec 0,\quad\mathbf{1}\{\|(\boldsymbol{I}+\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(z)\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\|>C_{0}\}\prec 0,\\
\mathbf{1}\{\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(z)\|>C_{0}\}\prec 0,\quad\mathbf{1}\{|1+N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(z)|<c_{0}\}\prec 0,\\
\mathbf{1}\{|1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(z)\boldsymbol{g}_{i}|<c_{0}\}\prec 0.\end{gathered}

Proof.

By conjugation symmetry, it suffices to show the statements for

z\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)
with
\operatorname{Im}z\geq 0
. Denote for simplicity

\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}=\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(z)
and
\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}=\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(z)
.
Let
\mathcal{S}_{N}^{(S)}=\mathrm{supp}({\mu_{N}^{(S)}})\cup\{0\}=\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}_{N}^{(S)}})\cup\{0\}
where
\mu_{N}^{(S)},\tilde{\mu}_{N}^{(S)}

are as defined in Lemma
21
.
Then Theorem
9
applied to
\boldsymbol{K}^{(S)}
guarantees that

\mathbf{1}\{\boldsymbol{K}^{(S)}\text{ has an eigenvalue outside }\mathcal{S}_{N}^{(S)}+(-\varepsilon/4,\varepsilon/4)\}\prec 0,

uniformly over all
S\subset[N]
with
|S|\leq L
. Note that

\mathcal{S}_{N}^{(S)}+(-\varepsilon/4,\varepsilon/4)\subseteq\mathcal{S}_{N}+(-\varepsilon/2,\varepsilon/2)
by
Lemma
21
. Then, applying the bound
\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}\|\leq 1/\textnormal{dist}(z,\mathcal{S}_{N}^{(S)})
and the condition
z\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)
, we get

\mathbf{1}\{\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}\|>2/\varepsilon\}\prec 0.

(C.50)

The remaining statements have already been shown for
z\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)
with

\operatorname{Im}z\geq N^{-1/11}
in (
C.48
).
For
z=x+i\eta
where
\eta\in[0,N^{-1/11}]
, define
z^{\prime}=x+iN^{-1/11}
.
On the event that
\boldsymbol{K}^{(S)}
has no eigenvalues
outside
\mathcal{S}_{N}+(-\varepsilon/2,\varepsilon/2)
, both
N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(z)
and

\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(z)=N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(z)+\gamma_{N}(-1/z)
are

C
-Lipschitz over
z\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)
for a constant
C=C(\varepsilon)>0
,
and
N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(z)\boldsymbol{g}_{i}
is
CN^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{g}_{i}\|^{2}
-Lipschitz where

N^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{g}_{i}\|^{2}\prec 1
by Assumption
5
. Then

N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(z)-N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(z^{\prime})\prec N^{-1/11},\quad\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(z)-\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(z^{\prime})\prec N^{-1/11},

N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(z)\boldsymbol{g}_{i}-N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(z^{\prime})\boldsymbol{g}_{i}\prec N^{-1/11},

so the remaining statements of the lemma hold also for
z\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)

with
\operatorname{Im}z\in[0,N^{-1/11}]
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
10
.

Again by conjugation symmetry, it suffices to show the result for

z\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)
with
\operatorname{Im}z\geq 0
. Denote for simplicity

\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}=\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(z)
and
\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}=\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(z)
.
The first estimate of Lemma
22
implies

\mathbf{1}\{\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}\|_{F}>C\sqrt{N}\}\prec 0,\qquad\mathbf{1}\{\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}>C\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}\}\prec 0

(C.51)

uniformly over
z\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)
and
\boldsymbol{A}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
. Then also, by
Assumption
5
(c) and (
C.33
) applied with

\boldsymbol{B}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}
,

\displaystyle 1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}

\displaystyle=1+N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}+O_{\prec}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(N^{-2}|1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}|^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\|_{F}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\|_{F}}}\right)

\displaystyle=1+N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}+O_{\prec}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(N^{-1/2}}}\right).

(C.52)

Let
d_{i}=d_{i,1}+d_{i,2}+d_{i,3}+d_{i,4}
be as defined in (
C.34
) with

\boldsymbol{A}=\boldsymbol{I}
. Then, applying (
C.51
), (
C.52
),
and the bounds of Lemma
22
, we obtain exactly as in the
proof of Lemma
20
(using again the fluctuation
averaging result of Lemma
13
) that,
uniformly over
z\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)
, we have
|d_{i,1}|,|d_{i,3}|,|d_{i,4}|\prec N^{-1}
,

|d_{i,2}|\prec N^{-1/2}
, and

|z_{N}(m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}})-z|\prec\frac{1}{N}\cdot\frac{1}{1+N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}d_{i,2}}}\right|+O_{\prec}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(N^{-1}}}\right)=O_{\prec}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(N^{-1}}}\right).

Fix any
\iota>0
. If
\operatorname{Im}z\geq N^{-1+\iota}
, then this
implies
\mathbf{1}\{z_{N}(m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}})\notin\mathbb{C}^{+}\}\prec 0
. By the same
stability argument as in Lemma
18
, we get

m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}(z)-\tilde{m}_{N}(z)\prec N^{-1}

uniformly over
z\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)
with
\operatorname{Im}z\geq N^{-1+\iota}
. For

\operatorname{Im}z\in[0,N^{-1+\iota}]
, on the event that all eigenvalues of
\boldsymbol{K}
belong
to
\mathcal{S}_{N}+(-\varepsilon/2,\varepsilon/2)
, we may apply that both
m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}(z)

and
\tilde{m}_{N}(z)
are
C(\varepsilon)
-Lipschitz over
z\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)
to
compare values at
z=x+i\eta
and
z^{\prime}=x+iN^{-1+\iota}
.
Applying
m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}(z^{\prime})-\tilde{m}_{N}(z^{\prime})\prec N^{-1}
, we then get for any

D>0
, all
z\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)
, some constant
C>0
, and all large
N
,

\mathbb{P}[|m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}(z)-\tilde{m}_{N}(z)|>CN^{-1+\iota}]\leq N^{-D}.

Since
\iota>0
is arbitrary, this shows

m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}(z)-\tilde{m}_{N}(z)\prec N^{-1}
uniformly over
z\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)
.
The bound
m_{\boldsymbol{K}}(z)-m_{N}(z)\prec N^{-1}
then follows from

m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}(z)=\gamma_{N}m_{\boldsymbol{K}}(z)+(1-\gamma_{N})(-1/z)
and

\tilde{m}_{N}(z)=\gamma_{N}m_{N}(z)+(1-\gamma_{N})(-1/z)
.

For the estimate of
\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{R}\boldsymbol{A}
, we apply the definition of

d_{i}=d_{i,1}+d_{i,2}+d_{i,3}+d_{i,4}
from (
C.34
)
and the identity (
C.31
) now with this matrix
\boldsymbol{A}
. Then
(
C.31
) gives

\operatorname{Tr}\Big[\boldsymbol{R}\boldsymbol{A}-({-}z\boldsymbol{I}-zm_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\boldsymbol{A}\Big]=\frac{1}{z}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{d_{i}}{1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}}.

Applying (
C.51
), (
C.52
),
and the bounds of Lemma
22
to
(
C.35
)–(
C.38
), uniformly over
z\in U_{N}(\varepsilon)
and
\boldsymbol{A}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
, we have
|d_{i,1}|,|d_{i,3}|,|d_{i,4}|\prec N^{-3/2}\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}
,
|d_{i,2}|\prec N^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}
, and hence

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{d_{i}}{1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(i)}\boldsymbol{g}_{i}}}}\right|\prec\frac{1}{1+N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}d_{i,2}}}\right|+O_{\prec}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(N^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}}}\right).

Finally, applying Lemma
13
with
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}=z\boldsymbol{I}
,

\Psi_{N}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})=C\sqrt{N}
, and
\Phi_{N}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma},\boldsymbol{A})=C\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}
(where we may
assume without loss of generality
\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}\in(N^{-\upsilon},N^{\upsilon})

by scale invariance of the desired estimate with respect to
\boldsymbol{A}
),
we get
|\sum_{i}d_{i,2}|\prec N^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}
. Thus,

\operatorname{Tr}\Big[\boldsymbol{R}\boldsymbol{A}-({-}z\boldsymbol{I}-zm_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\boldsymbol{A}\Big]\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\,\|\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}.

∎

Appendix D
Analysis of spiked eigenstructure

We now consider the asymptotic setup of Appendix
B.2
and
prove Corollary
11
and Theorem
12
.
As all the desired
statements are invariant under conjugation of
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}
by an orthogonal matrix,
we may assume without loss of generality that
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}
is diagonal and of the
form

\boldsymbol{\Sigma}=\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{0}\end{pmatrix},\qquad\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}=\mathop{\mathrm{}}{diag}(\lambda_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}),\ldots,\lambda_{r}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma})),\qquad\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{0}=\mathop{\mathrm{}}{diag}(\lambda_{r+1}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}),\ldots,\lambda_{n}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma})).

Denote the block decomposition of
\boldsymbol{G}
corresponding to

\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r},\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{0}
as

\boldsymbol{G}=[\boldsymbol{G}_{r},\boldsymbol{G}_{0}],\qquad\boldsymbol{G}_{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times r},\qquad\boldsymbol{G}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times(n-r)}.

We remind the reader that
\boldsymbol{G}_{r}
and
\boldsymbol{G}_{0}
need not be independent.

D.1
No outliers outside the limit support

We consider first the setting of
r=0
, and prove Corollary
11

together with some uniform convergence properties of

\tilde{m}_{N}
and
z_{N}
that will be used in the later analysis.

Recall the domain
\mathcal{T}_{N}
and function
z_{N}:\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathcal{T}_{N}\to\mathbb{C}
from
(
C.25
) and (
C.26
), and their asymptotic
analogues
\mathcal{T}
and
z:\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathcal{T}\to\mathbb{C}
from (
A.3
) and (
A.4
).

Lemma 23
.

Suppose Assumption
5
holds, and Assumption
6
holds
with
r=0
. Then, as
N\to\infty
,

(a)

z_{N}(\tilde{m})
and its derivative
z_{N}^{\prime}(\tilde{m})
converge uniformly
over compact subsets of
\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathcal{T}
to
z(\tilde{m})
and
z^{\prime}(\tilde{m})
.

(b)

For any
\varepsilon>0
and all large
N
,

\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}_{N}})\subseteq\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}})+(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon).

(c)

\tilde{m}_{N}(z)
and its derivative
\tilde{m}_{N}^{\prime}(z)
converge uniformly
over compact subsets of
\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}})
to
\tilde{m}(z)
and

\tilde{m}^{\prime}(z)
.

Proof.

For part (a), let
K\subset\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathcal{T}
be any fixed compact set.
Then
K
does not intersect some sufficiently small open neighborhood of the compact
domain
\mathcal{T}
. If Assumption
6
holds with
r=0
, then
\mathcal{T}_{N}
is
contained in this open neighborhood of
\mathcal{T}
for all large
N
, so
K\subset\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathcal{T}_{N}
, and both
z_{N}
and

z
are well-defined on
K
. The pointwise convergences
z_{N}(\tilde{m})\to z(\tilde{m})
and
z_{N}^{\prime}(\tilde{m})\to z^{\prime}(\tilde{m})
on
K
then follow from
\gamma_{N}\to\gamma
, the weak convergence
\nu_{N}\to\nu
, and the uniform boundedness of the
functions
\lambda\mapsto\lambda/(1+\lambda\tilde{m})

and
\lambda\mapsto\lambda^{2}/(1+\lambda\tilde{m})^{2}
on an open
neighborhood of
\mathrm{supp}({\nu})
, for
\tilde{m}\in K
. This convergence is
furthermore uniform because
\{z_{N}\}
and
\{z_{N}^{\prime}\}
are both equicontinuous
over
K
.

For part (b), consider any
x\notin\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}})+(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)
.
Then
[x-\varepsilon/2,x+\varepsilon/2]\subset\mathbb{R}\setminus\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}})
, so
\tilde{m}
is
well-defined and increasing on
[x-\varepsilon/2,x+\varepsilon/2]
. Let
[\tilde{m}_{-},\tilde{m}_{+}]=[\tilde{m}(x-\varepsilon/2),\tilde{m}(x+\varepsilon/2)]
. Then by Proposition
8
,

z^{\prime}(\tilde{m})>0
for all
\tilde{m}\in[\tilde{m}_{-},\tilde{m}_{+}]
, and

z([\tilde{m}_{-},\tilde{m}_{+}])=[x-\varepsilon/2,x+\varepsilon/2]
. The uniform convergence in
part (a) implies for all large
N
that
z_{N}(\tilde{m}_{-})<x
,

z_{N}(\tilde{m}_{+})>x
, and
z_{N}^{\prime}(\tilde{m})>0
for all
\tilde{m}\in[\tilde{m}_{-},\tilde{m}_{+}]
. Then there exists
\tilde{m}\in[\tilde{m}_{-},\tilde{m}_{+}]

where
z_{N}(\tilde{m})=x
and
z_{N}^{\prime}(\tilde{m})>0
, implying by
Proposition
8
that
x\notin\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}_{N}})
. So

\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}_{N}})\subseteq\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}})+(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)
as desired.

For part (c), let
K\subset\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}})
be any
fixed compact set. Then
K
does not intersect some sufficiently small open
neighborhood of the compact set
\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}})
, so the inclusion of part
(b) implies
K\subset\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}_{N}})
for all large
N
, and
both
\tilde{m}_{N}
and
\tilde{m}
are well-defined on
K
. The
uniform convergence
\tilde{m}_{N}(z)\to\tilde{m}(z)
and
\tilde{m}_{N}^{\prime}(z)\to\tilde{m}^{\prime}(z)
on
K
then follow from the weak convergence
\tilde{\mu}_{N}\to\tilde{\mu}
, the uniform boundedness of the functions
\lambda\mapsto 1/(\lambda-z)
and
\lambda\mapsto 1/(\lambda-z)^{2}
on an open neighborhood of

\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}})
for
z\in K
,
and the equicontinuity of
\{\tilde{m}_{N}\}
and
\{\tilde{m}_{N}^{\prime}\}
on
K
.
∎

Proof of Corollary
11
.

By Lemma
23
(b), for any fixed
\varepsilon>0
, we have

\mathcal{S}_{N}+(-\varepsilon/2,\varepsilon/2)\subseteq\mathcal{S}+(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)

for all large
N
. Then by Theorem
9
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}\{\boldsymbol{K}\text{ has an eigenvalue in }\mathbb{R}\setminus(\mathcal{S}+(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon))

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{1}\{\boldsymbol{K}\text{ has an eigenvalue in }\mathbb{R}\setminus(\mathcal{S}_{N}+(-\varepsilon/2,\varepsilon/2))\}\prec 0.

∎

D.2
Deterministic equivalents for generalized resolvents

We next introduce two generalized resolvents for the matrix
\boldsymbol{K}
, and extend
Theorem
10
to establish
deterministic equivalents for these generalized resolvents.

Define the spectral domain

U(\varepsilon)=\Big\{z\in\mathbb{C}:|z|\leq\varepsilon^{-1},\;\textnormal{dist}(z,\mathcal{S})\geq\varepsilon\Big\}

where
\mathcal{S}
is the limit support set defined in (
B.4
).
Given
z\in U(\varepsilon)
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{C}
, define a diagonal matrix

\boldsymbol{\Gamma}:=\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(z,\alpha)=z\boldsymbol{I}_{n}+\alpha\boldsymbol{V}_{r}\boldsymbol{V}_{r}^{\top}=\begin{pmatrix}(z+\alpha)\boldsymbol{I}_{r}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&z\boldsymbol{I}_{n-r}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n},\quad\boldsymbol{V}_{r}=\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{I}_{r}\\
\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}.

(D.1)

Define the first generalized resolvent

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}(z,\alpha)=\begin{pmatrix}-\boldsymbol{\Gamma}&\boldsymbol{G}^{\top}\\
\boldsymbol{G}&-\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\in\mathbb{C}^{(n+N)\times(n+N)}.

(D.2)

This matrix inverse exists if and only if
the Schur complement
\boldsymbol{G}^{\top}\boldsymbol{G}-\boldsymbol{\Gamma}=\boldsymbol{K}-\boldsymbol{\Gamma}

for its lower right block is invertible, in which case the upper-left block of

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}(z,\alpha)
is
\boldsymbol{R}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})=(\boldsymbol{K}-\boldsymbol{\Gamma})^{-1}
. The following
provides a deterministic equivalent for this block of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}(z,\alpha)
.

Lemma 24
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
12
, for any fixed
\varepsilon>0
,
there exist
C_{0},\alpha_{0}>0
(depending on
\varepsilon
) such that fixing any
\alpha\in\mathbb{C}
with
|\alpha|>\alpha_{0}
, the following hold:

(a)

The event

\mathcal{E}=\Big\{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}(z,\alpha)\text{ exists and }\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}(z,\alpha)\|\leq C_{0}\text{ for all }z\in U(\varepsilon)\Big\}

satisfies
\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{E}^{c}\}\prec 0
.

(b)

Uniformly over
z\in U(\varepsilon)

and deterministic unit vectors
\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\top}&\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}(z,\alpha)\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}\\
\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}+\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\top}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}+z\cdot\tilde{m}_{N,0}(z)\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}\right)^{-1}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}}}}\right\|\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}.

(D.3)

In the setting of Theorem
12
(c), let

\boldsymbol{u}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}(u_{1},\ldots,u_{N})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
be the
additional given vector for which
\{(u_{j},\boldsymbol{g}_{j}^{\top})\}_{j=1}^{N}
are
independent vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
. For
z\in U(\varepsilon)
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{C}
, define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{E}[u^{2}]&\mathbb{E}[u\boldsymbol{g}]^{\top}\\
\mathbb{E}[u\boldsymbol{g}]&\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{(n+1)\times(n+1)},

(D.4)

\displaystyle\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(z,\alpha)=\begin{pmatrix}z+\alpha&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{C}^{(n+1)\times(n+1)}

where
\mathbb{E}[u^{2}]
and
\mathbb{E}[u\boldsymbol{g}]
denote the common values of
\mathbb{E}[u_{j}^{2}]
and

\mathbb{E}[u_{j}\boldsymbol{g}_{j}]
for
j=1,\ldots,N
. Define the second generalized resolvent

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}}(z,\alpha)=\begin{pmatrix}{-}\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}&[\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{G}]^{\top}\\
[\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{G}]&-\boldsymbol{I}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}{-}(z+\alpha)&\mathbf{0}&\boldsymbol{u}^{\top}\\
\mathbf{0}&-\boldsymbol{\Gamma}&\boldsymbol{G}^{\top}\\
\boldsymbol{u}&\boldsymbol{G}&-\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\in\mathbb{C}^{(n+1+N)\times(n+1+N)}.

(D.5)

We have the following
deterministic equivalent for the upper-left block of
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}}(z,\alpha)
,
which is analogous to Lemma
24
.

Lemma 25
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
12
(c), for any fixed
\varepsilon>0
, there
exist
C_{0},\alpha_{0}>0
(depending on
\varepsilon
) such that fixing any
\alpha\in\mathbb{C}
with
|\alpha|>\alpha_{0}
, the following hold:

(a)

The event

\widetilde{}\mathcal{E}=\Big\{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}}(z,\alpha)\text{ exists and }\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}}(z,\alpha)\|\leq C_{0}\text{ for all }z\in U(\varepsilon)\Big\}

satisfies
\mathbf{1}\{\widetilde{}\mathcal{E}^{c}\}\prec 0
.

(b)

Uniformly over
z\in U(\varepsilon)
and
deterministic unit vectors
\boldsymbol{v}_{1},\boldsymbol{v}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
,

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\top}&\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}}(z,\alpha)\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}\\
\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}+\boldsymbol{v}_{1}^{\top}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}+z\cdot\tilde{m}_{N,0}(z)\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}\right)^{-1}\boldsymbol{v}_{2}}}}\right\|\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}.

(D.6)

In the remainder of this section, we prove Lemmas
24

and
25
. Recall

\mu_{N,0}=\rho_{\gamma_{N,0}}^{\textnormal{MP}}\boxtimes\nu_{N,0},\qquad\tilde{\mu}_{N,0}=\gamma_{N,0}\mu_{N,0}+(1-\gamma_{N,0})\delta_{0}.

Define the bulk components of the sample covariance and Gram matrices

\boldsymbol{K}_{0}=\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{\top}\boldsymbol{G}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{(n-r)\times(n-r)},\qquad\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{K}_{0}=\boldsymbol{G}_{0}\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}.

(D.7)

Define also the
N
-dependent bulk spectral support and spectral domain

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{N,0}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{supp}({\mu_{N,0}})\cup\{0\}=\mathrm{supp}({\tilde{\mu}_{N,0}})\cup\{0\},

\displaystyle U_{N,0}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\{z\in\mathbb{C}:|z|\leq\varepsilon^{-1},\textnormal{dist}(z,\mathcal{S}_{N,0})\geq\varepsilon\}.

(D.8)

Lemma
23
(b) shows

\mathcal{S}_{N,0}\subseteq\mathcal{S}+(-\varepsilon/2,\varepsilon/2)
for any fixed
\varepsilon>0
and all large
N
,
so also
U(\varepsilon)\subseteq U_{N,0}(\varepsilon/2)
for all large
N
. Thus, the results of
Appendix
C
applied to
\boldsymbol{K}_{0}
, which hold uniformly over

z\in U_{N,0}(\varepsilon/2)
for any fixed
\varepsilon>0
,
also hold uniformly over
z\in U(\varepsilon)
. In particular,
the following is an immediate consequence of Corollary
11
and
Theorem
10
,
which we record here for future reference.

Lemma 26
.

Suppose Assumptions
5
and
6
hold.
Then for any fixed
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}\{\boldsymbol{K}_{0}\text{ has an eigenvalue outside }\mathcal{S}+(-\varepsilon,\varepsilon)\}\prec 0.

Furthermore, uniformly over
z\in U(\varepsilon)
,

m_{\boldsymbol{K}_{0}}-m_{N,0}(z)\prec 1/N,\quad m_{\tilde{}\boldsymbol{K}_{0}}-\tilde{m}_{N,0}(z)\prec 1/N.

We now check that for sufficiently large
|\alpha|
, the generalized resolvent

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}(z,\alpha)
exists and has bounded operator norm with high probability.

Proof of Lemma
24
(a).

Let

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\text{ all eigenvalues of }\boldsymbol{K}_{0}\text{ belong to }\mathcal{S}+(-\varepsilon/2,\varepsilon/2),\text{ and }\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\boldsymbol{G}}}}\right\|<\sqrt{B}}}\right\}.

By Assumption
5
(b) and Lemma
26
,

\mathbf{1}\{{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}^{c}\}\prec 0
, so it suffices to show
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
.
On this event
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
, for any
z\in U(\varepsilon)
, we have that each
eigenvalue of
\boldsymbol{K}_{0}
is separated by at least
\varepsilon/2
from
z
.
Then,

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}_{0}(z):=\begin{pmatrix}-z\boldsymbol{I}_{n-r}&\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{\top}\\
\boldsymbol{G}_{0}&-\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\in\mathbb{C}^{(n-r+N)\times(n-r+N)}

(D.9)

exists for all
z\in U(\varepsilon)

because the Schur complement
\boldsymbol{K}_{0}-z\boldsymbol{I}_{n-r}
of its lower-right block
is invertible.
Furthermore, denoting
\boldsymbol{R}_{0}=(\boldsymbol{K}_{0}-z\boldsymbol{I}_{n-r})^{-1}
, we have

\|\boldsymbol{R}_{0}\|\leq 2/\varepsilon
and
\|\boldsymbol{G}_{0}\|\leq\|\boldsymbol{G}\|<\sqrt{B}
, so

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}_{0}(z)\|=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{R}_{0}&\boldsymbol{R}_{0}\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{\top}\\
\boldsymbol{G}_{0}\boldsymbol{R}_{0}&\boldsymbol{G}_{0}\boldsymbol{R}_{0}\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{\top}-\boldsymbol{I}_{N}\end{pmatrix}}}\right\|\leq C_{1}

(D.10)

for some constant
C_{1}
depending only on
\varepsilon,B
.

Now write
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}(z,\alpha)
as defined in (
D.2
) in its block
decomposition with blocks of sizes
r
and
n-r+N
. Then the Schur complement
of the upper left block of size
r\times r
is given by

\boldsymbol{S}=-\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}&\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}_{0}(z)\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}\\
\boldsymbol{G}_{r}\end{pmatrix}-(\alpha+z)\boldsymbol{I}_{r}.

(D.11)

Notice that

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{S}^{*}=~

\displaystyle|\alpha+z|^{2}\boldsymbol{I}_{r}+\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}&\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}_{0}(z)\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}\\
\boldsymbol{G}_{r}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}&\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\overline{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}_{0}(z)}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}\\
\boldsymbol{G}_{r}\end{pmatrix}

(D.12)

\displaystyle+(\bar{\alpha}+\bar{z})\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}&\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}_{0}(z)\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}\\
\boldsymbol{G}_{r}\end{pmatrix}+(\alpha+z)\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}&\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\overline{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}_{0}(z)}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}\\
\boldsymbol{G}_{r}\end{pmatrix}

(D.13)

where the first two terms are positive semi-definite.
Therefore, applying (
D.10
) and
\|\boldsymbol{G}_{r}\|\leq\|\boldsymbol{G}\|<\sqrt{B}
on the event
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
,
there exist
\alpha_{0},c_{0}>0
depending only on
\varepsilon,B
, such that

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{S}^{*})\geq|\alpha+z|^{2}-2(|\alpha|+|z|)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\boldsymbol{G}_{r}}}}\right\|^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}_{0}(z)}}}\right\|>c_{0}

(D.14)

for any
z\in U(\varepsilon)
and
|\alpha|>\alpha_{0}
. Consequently, under the event

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
, the Schur complement
\boldsymbol{S}
in (
D.11
) is
invertible with
\|\boldsymbol{S}^{-1}\|<c_{0}^{-1/2}
.
Then
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}(z,\alpha)
exists, and

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}(z,\alpha)\|

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{S}^{-1}&-\boldsymbol{S}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}&\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}_{0}(z)\\
-\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}_{0}(z)\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}\\
\boldsymbol{G}_{r}\end{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{S}^{-1}&\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}_{0}(z)+\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}_{0}(z)\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}\\
\boldsymbol{G}_{r}\end{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{S}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}&\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}_{0}(z)\end{pmatrix}}}\right\|\leq C_{0}

(D.15)

for a constant
C_{0}>0
depending only on
\varepsilon,B
.
This shows
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
as desired.
∎

For the matrix
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}=\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(z,\alpha)
in (
D.1
), recall the definitions of

\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})
and
\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})
from
(
C.1
). The following provides an analogue of
Lemma
22
for these quantities.

Lemma 27
.

Fix any
\varepsilon>0
and
L\geq 1
. Then there exist
C_{0},c_{0},\alpha_{0}>0
such that
for any fixed
\alpha\in\mathbb{C}
with
|\alpha|>\alpha_{0}
,
uniformly over
S\subset[N]
with
|S|\leq L
, over
j\in S
, and over

z\in U(\varepsilon)
,

\begin{gathered}\mathbf{1}\{|\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})|>C_{0}\}\prec 0,\quad\mathbf{1}\{|\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})|<c_{0}\}\prec 0,\quad\mathbf{1}\{\|(z^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}+\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\boldsymbol{\Sigma})^{-1}\|>C_{0}\}\prec 0,\\
\mathbf{1}\{\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\|>C_{0}\}\prec 0,\quad\mathbf{1}\{|1+N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})|<c_{0}\}\prec 0,\\
\mathbf{1}\{|1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\boldsymbol{g}_{j}|<c_{0}\}\prec 0.\end{gathered}

Proof.

Suppose
|\alpha|
is large enough so that Lemma
24
(a)
holds. Since
\boldsymbol{R}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})
is the upper-left block of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{R}}(z,\alpha)
,
Lemma
24
(a) applied with
\boldsymbol{G}^{(S)}
in place of
\boldsymbol{G}

shows that
\mathbf{1}\{\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\|>C_{0}\}\prec 0
for a constant
C_{0}>0
,
uniformly over
S\subset[N]
with
|S|\leq L
and over
z\in U(\varepsilon)
.
For the remaining statements,
let
\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{(S)}\in\mathbb{R}^{(N-|S|)\times(n-r)}
be the submatrix of
\boldsymbol{G}_{0}
with
the rows of
S
removed, and define

\boldsymbol{K}_{0}^{(S)}={\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{(S)}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{(S)},\quad\widetilde{}\boldsymbol{K}_{0}^{(S)}={\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{(S)}}{\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{(S)}}^{\top},

\boldsymbol{R}_{0}^{(S)}=(\boldsymbol{K}_{0}^{(S)}-z\boldsymbol{I}_{n-r})^{-1},\quad m_{\boldsymbol{K}_{0}}^{(S)}=\frac{1}{n-r}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{R}_{0}^{(S)},\quad\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}_{0}}^{(S)}=\gamma_{N,0}m_{\boldsymbol{K}_{0}}^{(S)}+(1-\gamma_{N,0})\Big({-}\frac{1}{z}\Big).

Then by Lemma
26
applied to
\boldsymbol{K}_{0}^{(S)}
, also

\mathbf{1}\{\|\boldsymbol{R}_{0}^{(S)}\|>C_{0}\}\prec 0
for a constant
C_{0}>0
.

Using these bounds, we first show the comparisons

|\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})-\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}_{0}}^{(S)}|\prec 1/N,\quad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})-N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{0}\boldsymbol{R}_{0}^{(S)}}}\right|\prec 1/N.

(D.16)

For the first comparison, notice that in the decompositions into blocks
of sizes
r
and
n-r
,

\frac{n-r}{n}\,m_{\boldsymbol{K}_{0}}^{(S)}=\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{Tr}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\boldsymbol{R}_{0}^{(S)}\end{pmatrix}

and

\displaystyle m_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{Tr}\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{(S)\top}\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{(S)}-(\alpha+z)\boldsymbol{I}_{r}&\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{(S)\top}\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{(S)}\\
\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{(S)\top}\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{(S)}&\boldsymbol{K}_{0}^{(S)}-z\boldsymbol{I}_{N-|S|}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{Tr}\begin{pmatrix}(\boldsymbol{S}_{r}^{(S)})^{-1}&-(\boldsymbol{S}_{r}^{(S)})^{-1}\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{(S)\top}\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{R}_{0}^{(S)}\\
-\boldsymbol{R}_{0}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{(S)\top}\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{S}_{r}^{(S)})^{-1}&\boldsymbol{R}_{0}^{(S)}+\boldsymbol{R}_{0}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{(S)\top}\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{S}_{r}^{(S)})^{-1}\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{(S)\top}\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{R}_{0}^{(S)}\end{pmatrix},

where

\boldsymbol{S}_{r}^{(S)}:=\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{(S)\top}\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{(S)}-(\alpha+z)\boldsymbol{I}_{r}-\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{(S)\top}\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{R}_{0}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{(S)\top}\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{(S)}

(D.17)

is the Schur complement of the lower-right block. We have

\lVert(\boldsymbol{S}_{r}^{(S)})^{-1}\rVert\leq\lVert\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\rVert\prec 1
,

\lVert\boldsymbol{R}_{0}^{(S)}\rVert\prec 1
,
and by Assumption
5
,
\lVert\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{(S)}\rVert\prec 1
and

\lVert\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{(S)}\rVert\prec 1
. Combining these bounds and using

|\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{A}|\leq r\|\boldsymbol{A}\|
when
\boldsymbol{A}
has rank
r
(as follows from the
von Neumann trace inequality),

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|m_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})-\frac{n-r}{n}\,m_{\boldsymbol{K}_{0}}^{(S)}}}\right|

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{Tr}\begin{pmatrix}(\boldsymbol{S}_{r}^{(S)})^{-1}&-(\boldsymbol{S}_{r}^{(S)})^{-1}\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{(S)\top}\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{R}_{0}^{(S)}\\
-\boldsymbol{R}_{0}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{(S)\top}\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{S}_{r}^{(S)})^{-1}&\boldsymbol{R}_{0}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{(S)\top}\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{S}_{r}^{(S)})^{-1}\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{(S)\top}\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{R}_{0}^{(S)}\end{pmatrix}}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}|\operatorname{Tr}(\boldsymbol{S}_{r}^{(S)})^{-1}|+\frac{1}{n}|\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{R}_{0}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{(S)\top}\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{S}_{r}^{(S)})^{-1}\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{(S)\top}\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{(S)}\boldsymbol{R}_{0}^{(S)}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r}{n}\lVert(\boldsymbol{S}_{r}^{(S)})^{-1}\rVert+\frac{r}{n}\lVert\boldsymbol{G}_{r}^{(S)}\rVert^{2}\lVert\boldsymbol{G}_{0}^{(S)}\rVert^{2}\lVert\boldsymbol{R}_{0}^{(S)}\rVert^{2}\lVert(\boldsymbol{S}_{r}^{(S)})^{-1}\rVert\prec 1/N.

Then also
|m_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})-m_{\boldsymbol{K}_{0}}^{(S)}|\prec 1/N
since

|m_{\boldsymbol{K}_{0}}^{(S)}|\leq\|\boldsymbol{R}_{0}^{(S)}\|\prec 1
and
(n-r)/n=1+O_{\prec}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1/N}}\right)
.
Hence
|\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})-\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}_{0}}^{(S)}|\prec 1/N

from the definitions
\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})=\gamma_{N}m_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})+(1-\gamma_{N})(-1/z)
and

\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}_{0}}^{(S)}=\gamma_{N,0}m_{\boldsymbol{K}_{0}}^{(S)}+(1-\gamma_{N,0})(-1/z)
,
as
|1/z|\leq\varepsilon
for
z\in U(\varepsilon)
and
\gamma_{N,0}=\gamma_{N}+O_{\prec}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1/N}}\right)
.
The proof of the second comparison of (
D.16
) is analogous,
considering in addition

\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{Tr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}-\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{0}\end{pmatrix}}}\right)\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})=\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{Tr}\begin{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{0}\end{pmatrix}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\leq\frac{r}{n}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}\|\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\|\prec\frac{1}{N}.

(D.18)

Now, Lemma
22
applied with
\boldsymbol{K}_{0}
shows, uniformly over
S\subset[N]
with
|S|\leq L
and over
z\in U(\varepsilon)
,

\mathbf{1}\{|\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}_{0}}^{(S)}|>C_{0}\}\prec 0,\quad\mathbf{1}\{|\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}_{0}}^{(S)}|<c_{0}\}\prec 0,\quad\mathbf{1}\{|1+N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{0}\boldsymbol{R}_{0}^{(S)}|<c_{0}\}\prec 0,

which together with (
D.16
) implies

\mathbf{1}\{|\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})|>C_{0}\}\prec 0,\quad\mathbf{1}\{|\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})|<c_{0}\}\prec 0,\quad\mathbf{1}\{|1+N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})|<c_{0}\}\prec 0

for adjusted constants
C_{0},c_{0}>0
.
Also by Assumption
5
, uniformly over
j\in S
,

N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\boldsymbol{g}_{j}-N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\prec N^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\|_{F}\leq N^{-1/2}\|\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\|\prec N^{-1/2},

(D.19)

so
\mathbf{1}\{|1+N^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{R}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\boldsymbol{g}_{j}|<c\}\prec 0

for a constant
c>0
.
Lastly, from the definition of
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}=\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(z,\alpha)
in (
D.1
), we have

\displaystyle z^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}+\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\boldsymbol{\Sigma}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{r}+(\frac{\alpha}{z}+1)\boldsymbol{I}_{r}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{0}+\boldsymbol{I}_{n-r}\end{pmatrix}.

(D.20)

By (
D.16
) and Lemma
22
, we have

\mathbf{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{0}+\boldsymbol{I}_{n-r}}}\right)^{-1}}}}\right\|>C}}\right\}\prec 0

(D.21)

for some constant
C>0
. We have already proved
\mathbf{1}\{|\tilde{m}_{\boldsymbol{K}}^{(S)}(\boldsymbol{\Gamma})|>C_{0}\}\prec 0
, and applying
